So there was a gap there at the beginning where it was just the cameras before the depression hit.

I mean, I do my job fine. It was just... I just knew they were watching me. It didn't have anything to do with me being worried about getting fired or messing up. It was just a fact that the cameras were on me and they were doing it on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Going back further—before those few weeks when you first noticed the camera monitoring, was there a longer period of time when you felt completely normal, with no worries about being watched and no depression either?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I used to have just steady day shifts, but then they switched me to rotating nights and started throwing in all this overtime. That's when everything started. So, no, there hasn't been a time recently where I felt completely normal before all this.\n\nDoctor: Let me make sure I have the timeline right. You said the camera monitoring started about 1 year and 5 months ago when your work schedule changed. The camera stuff started right when they changed my schedule, about a year and five months ago. But I didn't start feeling really down and exhausted until... well, I guess the depression and just losing interest in everything has been with me for about a year and two months. I mean... to me, the cameras were real. I didn't feel like my mind was playing tricks on me or anything like that. I knew what my own thoughts were, and I just knew they were watching me. It actually got bad enough at work that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation. That was after I admitted I was having thoughts about suicide. I can literally see people getting lost while I'm talking to them. It wasn't like that at first, but it's really bad now.\n\nDoctor: During those first few weeks when you started noticing the cameras watching you—before the depression began—did you feel like you had more energy than usual, need less sleep, or feel like you could accomplish a lot of things at once? I didn't feel like I could do a bunch of things at once, and I was sleeping the same as always. I didn't have extra energy or anything.\n\nDoctor: When you first started noticing the cameras watching you those few weeks before the depression began—during that time when you were feeling emotionally okay—did you have any other strange experiences or beliefs about yourself or the world around you that felt unusual, even if they weren't about being watched at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it was just the cameras. I didn't have any other weird thoughts or, you know, strange experiences like that.
